Parkside Community Child Care Centre OSHC (SE-00010779)

Address: 12 Robsart, Street, Parkside SA 5063
Approved Provider: Community Child Care Centre Inc is the approved provider (PR-00006056)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Regulation 92 – Medication record 
  • Regulation 97(3) - Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 103 - Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair
  • Regulation 170 - Policies and procedures to be followed

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 12 October 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Ensure medication documentation and medical management plans are current, accurate and consistent by:
    a) conducting a service-wide audit of medication records, medical management plans, risk minimisation plans and communication plans to identify and rectify any missing, expired or inconsistent documentation
    b) confirming that medication labels, prescribed dosages and administration instructions are accurate and consistent with relevant medical documentation
    c) obtaining updated medical management plans where existing plans have expired and updating associated risk minimisation and communication plans, where required
    d) implementing a process to regularly review and maintain medical management documentation to ensure it remains current and accurate
    e) ensuring relevant educators are informed of and have access to current medical management plans and medication administration instructions.
  2. Ensure emergency and evacuation procedures are regularly rehearsed and documented in accordance with Regulation 97(3) by:
    a) developing and implementing a schedule to ensure emergency and evacuation procedures are rehearsed at least once every three months
    b) conducting and documenting an emergency and evacuation rehearsal in accordance with the service's emergency procedures
    c) maintaining records of all rehearsals, including the date, type of emergency rehearsed and any identified improvements.
  3. Ensure the service premises are maintained in a safe condition and good repair by:
    a) engaging a suitably qualified person to repair the protruding sharp metal edge at the base of the storage shed near the gymnasium
    b) engaging a suitably qualified person to repair the protruding sharp metal edge on the Colorbond fence panel adjacent to the storage shed
    c) preventing children's access to the identified hazards until the repairs have been completed.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Berri Regional Child Care Centre Inc (SE-00010169)

Address: 1B Fielder Street, Berri SA 5343
Approved Provider: The Management Committee, Berri Regional Child Care Centre Inc (PR-
00006009)

Details:
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider hascontravened:

  • Section 167 (1) – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards
  • Regulation 170 – Policies and procedures to be followed

Action Taken:
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 1 September 2026, requiring the
following by a specified time:

  1. Provide evidence that all cots used at the service comply with the service’s Sleep and Rest Policy, which requires cots to meet current Australian Standards (AS/NZS 2172:2013).
  2. Provide evidence that all bedding in the under 2-year-old sleep room is fitted, with no loose or excess material.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

McKay Children’s Centre (SE-00012153)  

Address: 7 Scott Street, PENOLA, SA, 5277

Approved Provider: McKay Children's Centre Inc (PR-00006212)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165 – Offence to inadequately supervise children 
  • Section 167 – Offence relating to the protection of children from harm and hazards
  • Regulation 92 (3)(f)  Medication record
  • Regulation 103 - Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 26 August 2026 requiring the following by a specified time:

  1. Review and implement the service's supervision arrangements to ensure children are actively supervised and accounted for at all times, including during transitions between indoor and outdoor environments.
  2. Ensure all educators complete training in active supervision, including supervision during transitions, and maintain records demonstrating completion of the training.
  3. Remove all children's resources from the outdoor resource storage shed and ensure the shed and all resources stored within it are thoroughly cleaned and sanitised before the resources are returned to use.
  4. Implement effective measures to prevent animals and pests from entering the outdoor resource storage shed and contaminating children's resources. 
  5. Remove all stagnant water and debris from tyres within the outdoor play environment and ensure tyres used for children's play are maintained in a clean, hygienic and safe condition.
  6. Repair or replace the retractable hose to ensure it retracts fully into its stored position and does not present a trip hazard to children.
  7. Remove all outdoor equipment and resources identified as unclean, deteriorated or in a state of disrepair until they are restored to a safe, clean and hygienic condition.
  8. Develop and implement a documented system for daily inspection and sign off by the Nominated Supervisor or Responsible Person, of the service premises furniture and equipment to identify, record and promptly rectify hazards and maintenance issues.
  9. Undertake an audit of all medication records for children attending the service and ensure:
    a) each medication record clearly identifies the dosage of medication to be administered in accordance with regulation 92(3)(f) of the National Regulations
    b) medication is not administered where the medication record does not clearly identify the dosage to be administered until the required information has been obtained and recorded
    c) any deficiencies identified through the audit are rectified.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Waikerie Primary School OSHC (SE-00011092)

Address: 23 Coombe Terrace, Waikerie SA 5330 
Approved Provider: Waikerie Primary School Council Inc (PR-00006436)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165 – Offence to inadequately supervise children
  • Section 167 – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards
  • Section 173 – Prescribed information to be displayed – education and care service other than a family day care service
  • Section 174(2)(c) – Offence to fail to notify certain information to Regulatory Authority
  • Regulation 75 – Information about educational program to be kept
  • Regulation 84B – Sleep and rest policies
  • Regulation 84C – Risk assessment for purposes of sleep and rest policies and procedures
  • Regulation 87 – Incident, injury, trauma and illness record 
  • Regulation 90 – Medical conditions policy
  • Regulation 97 – Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 102AAC – Risk assessment for the purposes of safe arrival of children policies and procedures
  • Regulation 103 – Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 4 September 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Provide evidence that supervision procedures are developed and implemented consistently, with strengthened arrangements to ensure all children remain within sight and hearing of an educator at all times (including during transitions, outdoor play, access to belongings, and signing in).
  2. Provide evidence that all sharp utensils, including knives, are stored securely and inaccessible to children.
  3. Provide evidence that all hazardous chemicals are stored securely and inaccessible to children.  
  4. Provide evidence that all prescribed information is displayed, including:
    a) the hours and days of operation of the education and care service;
    b) the name and position of the responsible person in charge of the education and care service at any given time; and 
    c) the current service approval certificate. 
  5. Submit a notification to the Regulatory Authority advising of any changes to the operating hours of the service.
  6. Provide evidence that the educational program is displayed.
  7. Provide revised Sleep and Rest Policy that includes all required elements, including:
    a) how requests from families regarding a child’s sleep, rest and cultural preferences are considered;
    b) supervision and monitoring during sleep and rest periods, including the method and frequency of safety checks, and the documentation of sleep and rest; 
    c) the induction, training and required knowledge of staff in relation to best practice for children’s sleep and rest; and
    d) communication of the service’s sleep and rest policies and procedures to parents. 
  8. Provide a Sleep and Rest risk assessment. 
  9. Provide an incident form that includes all required information, including:
    a) the details of any person who witnessed the incident, injury or trauma; 
    b) the name of the person notified of the incident; and
    c) the time and date that the notification occurred.
  10. Provide a revised Medical Conditions Policy that includes all required elements, including:
    a) requiring the development of risk-minimisation plans in consultation with the parents of the child; and 
    b) requiring the development of communication plans. 
  11. Provide evidence of an Emergency Rehearsal procedure that outlines the process for documenting and evaluating emergency rehearsals. 
  12. Provide a risk assessment identifying potential emergencies. 
  13. Provide evidence that the rear emergency exit signage is visible and identifiable at all times, including when the door is in an open position.  
  14. Provide a risk assessment outlining how risks to children’s safety, wellbeing and supervision during travel and arrival are identified, assessed and minimised. 
  15. Provide evidence that the service premises are maintained in good repair, including external timber slatting.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

The Community Child Care Centre at Flinders Medical Centre (SE-00010259)

Address: Flinders Medical Centre, Flinders Drive, BEDFORD PARK SA 5042

Approved Provider: Community Child Care Centre at Flinders Medical Centre (PR-00006055)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 167 (1) Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards
  • Section 172 Offence to fail to display prescribed information
  • Section 173 (2)(c) Offence to fail to notify certain circumstances to Regulatory Authority
  • Section 174 (2)(b)– Offence to fail to notify certain information to Regulatory Authority 
  • Regulation 103 (1) – Premises, furniture, equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair. 
  • Regulation 170 – Policies and procedures to be followed 

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 10 September 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Remove all chemicals from the nappy change area in the Baby House room and ensure chemicals are stored securely and inaccessible to children.
  2. Ensure all prescribed information displayed at the service, including the service rating, accurately reflects the current service information.
  3. Submit the required SA12 notification relating to the excavation works and ensure the excavation area remains securely barricaded and inaccessible to children until works are completed.
  4. Conduct an audit of all complaints and incidents from the previous 12 months, submit any required retrospective notifications to the Regulatory Authority, and provide the Education Standards Board with the outcome of the audit, including details of any notifications made.
  5. Replace the deteriorated cots in the Baby House and ensure all cot sheets are securely fitted to mattresses and maintained in accordance with safe sleep requirements.
  6. Replace the mat used as soft fall in the Baby House outdoor area with compliant impact-absorbing surfacing and ensure fall zones are adequately protected.
  7. Clean and maintain the sandpit in the Baby House outdoor area and ensure it is free from leaves, twigs and other debris.
  8. Secure the outdoor shed in the Baby House yard, ensure equipment and materials are stored safely, neatly and in an organised manner.
  9. Remove or replace the deteriorated couches in the Toddler 2 room and ensure all furniture is maintained in a hygienic condition.
  10. Remove, repair or replace the damaged and deteriorated books in the Toddler 2 and Kindy rooms to ensure resources are appropriate and maintained in good condition.
  11. Remove or replace the deteriorated table in the Kindy room and ensure all furniture surfaces are safe, clean and maintained in good repair.
  12. Repair the damaged bathroom partitions in the Kindy room and ensure all surfaces can be effectively cleaned and sanitised.
  13. Remove or replace the deteriorated child-sized vehicle play units in the Kindy room and ensure all play equipment is maintained in a safe condition.
  14. Remove or replace the deteriorated outdoor play kitchen and sink units in the Toddler 2 yard, remove and replace the soiled pillow, and ensure all outdoor equipment and resources are maintained in good repair and hygienic condition.  
  15. Professionally clean or replace the stained carpet in the Kindy room and ensure floor coverings are maintained in a clean and hygienic condition.
  16. Remove spider webs from the Toddler 1 and Toddler 2 outdoor play yards, including on perimeter fencing and implement regular cleaning and maintenance practices to ensure outdoor environments remain clean and safe.
  17. Ensure all medications are stored and labelled in accordance with the service's Medical Conditions Policy.  

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Lake Albert Learning Centre (SE-00010556) 

Address: 21 Bonney Street, MENINGIE, SA 5264

Approved Provider: The Trustee for JAK's Family Trust (PR-40007188)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 167 – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards 
  • Regulation 92 – Medication record 
  • Regulation 103 - Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair
  • Regulation 112 – Nappy change facilities
  • Regulation 114 – Outdoor space – shade
  • Regulation 170 – Policies and procedures to be followed

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 28 August 2026 requiring the following by a specified time:

  1. Remove the stacked crates adjacent to the fence and ensure play equipment is positioned and maintained so that it does not create an unstable climbing or fall hazard. 
  2. Relocate the trellis structures away from the boundary fence to prevent children using them to climb or gain access to the boundary fence. 
  3. Replace the ill-fitting cot sheets with correctly sized sheets that fit securely to the nursery mattresses without loose or excess material. 
  4. Remove the deteriorated and unstable sink from the outdoor environment to eliminate the identified injury hazard. 
  5. Clean and remediate the affected shade sail to remove the identified mould and implement a documented cleaning and maintenance schedule to ensure outdoor shade structures are maintained in a clean and safe condition.
  6. Rectify the sandpit by adding sufficient sand to eliminate voids within the tyre-lined perimeter that may provide harbourage for spiders, insects or other pests. 
  7. Rectify the tyre planter boxes by adding sufficient soil to eliminate voids within the tyres that may provide harbourage for spiders, insects or other pests. 
  8. Replace the damaged nappy change mat with a change mat that is intact and capable of being effectively cleaned and sanitised. 
  9. Remove the deteriorated bookshelf from the under-two-years outdoor play area.
  10. Remove all leaves, gum nuts and other debris from the artificial turf within the under-two-years yard and implement a documented cleaning and maintenance schedule to ensure the area is routinely inspected and maintained in a clean and safe condition, free from accessible choking hazards.
  11. Cease changing children on a mat placed on the floor within the toilet traffic area and implement an appropriate and hygienic nappy-changing arrangement for older children. 
  12. Provide adequate shade within the outdoor play yard used by children aged under two years to protect children from overexposure to ultraviolet radiation and heat.
  13. Review and rectify the medical condition and medication documentation for all affected children to ensure:
    a) current risk minimisation and communication plans are developed and maintained for each child diagnosed with a medical condition, where required;
    b) medication records accurately identify the dosage of medication to be administered and are consistent with the medication label and relevant medical management plan;
    c) any discrepancies identified between medication documentation and medication held at the service are rectified before the medication is administered.
  14. Engage KidSafe to undertake a comprehensive assessment of the Nursery/Toddler outdoor play area. The assessment must specifically consider the suitability of the surface material and other environmental features for children under two years of age, including any potential choking hazards. The Approved Provider must provide the Regulatory Authority with the assessment, and a written action plan that addresses recommendations made within the assessment, including clear timeframes for implementation. 

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Barossa Recreation & Fitness Centre Vacation Care (SE-00010162) 

Service Address: Magnolia Road, TANUNDA, SA, 5352

Approved Provider: BELGRAVIA HEALTH & LEISURE GROUP PTY LTD ATF BELGRAVIA LEISURE UNIT TRUST (PR-00002210)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165 - Offence to inadequately supervise children
  • Section 167 - Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards 
  • Regulation 84C - Risk assessment for sleep and rest
  • Regulation 92 - Medication record 
  • Regulation 95 - Procedure for administration of medication
  • Regulation 97 - Emergency and evacuation procedures (multiple)
  • Regulation 101 - Conduct of risk assessment for excursion
  • Regulation 102 Authorisation for excursions
  • Regulation 103 - Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair
  • Regulation 117A - Placing a person in day-to-day charge
  • Regulation 118 - Educational leader
  • Regulation 168 - Policies and Procedures (multiple)
  • Regulation 170 - Policies and procedures to be followed
  • Regulation 173 - Prescribed information to be displayed—education and care service other than a family day care service

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 11 August 2026, requiring the following by a specific date: 

  1. Provide a statement regarding current plans on how the service will manage children using public toilets and ensuring they are adequately supervised at all times. 
  2. Develop risk assessments on the contextual service risk and the subsequent effective risk mitigation strategies. 
  3. Provide an updated and strengthened regular outing risk assessment for the “Rex Pool”.
  4. Develop a regular outing risk assessment for the local primary school outdoor space. 
  5. Provide updated authorisations for both regular outings and the services excursions and a statement on how authorisations will be gained ongoing. 
  6. Provide a statement on how educators will manage medication going forward to ensure children are attending with the medication that has been prescribed by a registered medical practitioner, from its original container, bearing the original label with the name of the child to whom the medication is to be administered, and before the expiry or use by date, with an in-date medical management plan, risk minimisation plan, communication plan and medication record.
  7. Implement an audit to ensure all medical management plan, risk minimisation plan, communication plan and medication record is in place for all children with medical conditions at the service and a plan in place ongoing.  
  8. Develop a potential emergency risk assessment that is contextual to the service 
  9. Complete an evacuation and invacuation rehearsal. 
  10. Provide a map of all spaces used by the service and the designated exits used by the service.
  11. Develop a sleep and rest risk assessment. 
  12. Develop a 12-month schedule to ensure emergency procedures are rehearsed within a 3-month period
  13. Develop an excursion policy 
  14. Update the delivery and collection policy and sleep and rest policy. 
  15. Provide the educational leader designation in writing
  16. Provide responsible person consent forms for all responsible people. 
  17. Display all required prescribed information. 
  18. Provide a statement on how the service will ensure the premises used by children is clean, safe and well maintained ongoing.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Busy Kids Streaky Bay (SE-40023529)

Address: 10 Mudge Tce, STREAKY BAY, SA, 5680

Approved Provider: BUSY KIDS STREAKY BAY PTY LTD (PR-40029055)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 167-Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards 
  • Regulation 95(a)(i) – Procedure for administration of medication
  • Regulation 97(3)(a) – Emergency and evacuation procedures 
  • Regulation 103- Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair 
  • Regulation 173(a)-Prescribed information to be displayed 
  • Regulation 175(a)-Prescribed information to be notified to Regulatory Authority 

Action Taken: 

Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 21 August 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Remove service iPads from bathroom and nappy change areas. Review and implement procedures for recording nappy changes that maintain hygiene standards and protect children's privacy and confidentiality.
  2. Audit current cot sheets and ensure all cot sheets are securely fitted to mattresses and regularly checked for safe fit. Review sleep and rest practices to ensure bedding does not create a risk to children.
  3. Repair, modify or replace garden bed planter boxes to eliminate sharp edges. 
  4. Repair or replace the water meter box cover to remove sharp edges and install an effective locking or securing mechanism to prevent child access and entrapment.
  5. Remove, cap or modify protruding metal fence points to eliminate impact and injury hazards. Ensure fencing within the education and care environment is maintained in a safe condition.
  6. Prevent children's access to the storage/shipping container. Secure the container and rectify exposed locking mechanisms, protrusions and pinch points to eliminate identified hazards.
  7. Install and maintain an effective child-resistant locking mechanism on the gate providing access to the roadway. Implement procedures to ensure perimeter gates remain secured during service operation.
  8. Review the suitability of the preschool outdoor environment for children under two years of age. Implement measures to eliminate or minimise choking and ingestion hazards and ensure the environment is appropriate for the ages of children attending.
  9. Obtain antihistamine medication in the original container bearing the original pharmacy label and the child's name. Review all medication held at the service to ensure compliance with medication administration requirements.
  10. Remove expired medication and obtain replacement medication that is within the expiry date. Implement a process to routinely monitor medication expiry dates.
  11. Conduct and document emergency rehearsals in accordance with regulatory requirements. Ensure emergency rehearsal records are maintained, readily accessible and available for inspection upon request.
  12. Repair, refurbish or remove the wooden boat play structure until peeling paint and splintering timber have been rectified. Implement regular inspections and maintenance of outdoor play equipment.
  13. Repair, replace or remove the outdoor table until peeling paint and rusted surfaces have been rectified. Ensure outdoor furniture is maintained in a safe condition and good repair.
  14. Update the prescribed information displayed at the service to accurately reflect the current hours and days of operation. Implement processes to ensure displayed information remains current.
  15. Review the service's approved operational information and notify the Regulatory Authority of any changes to the service's days or hours of operation. Ensure information recorded in NQA ITS accurately reflects the service's operations.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Margaret Ives Community Children's Centre Norwood (SE-00010607)

Address: 19 Edward Street Norwood SA 5067 

Approved Provider: Margaret Ives Community Children's Centre Inc PR-00006975 

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Regulation 103 - Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair
  • Section 167(2) – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazard 

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 2 September 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Store the sandpit cover so it is not accessible to children and does not present an entanglement hazard.
  2. Secure, modify or remove the wooden storage unit to eliminate the risk of finger entrapment.
  3. Remove or store the blue tarp so it is not accessible to children.
  4. Relocate all climbing equipment away from the perimeter fence and install adequate impact-absorbing surfacing to minimise the risk of injury to children.
  5. Repair, replace or remove all damaged and deteriorated furniture and equipment to ensure it is safe for use by children.
  6. Remove the smashed planter pot from the environment and replace it if required.
  7. Repair, replace or remove damaged protective padding and deteriorated outdoor furnishings and secure the hose hanging over the washing line to eliminate potential hazards. 
  8. Implement and maintain a documented daily safety check. The check must be completed before children access the service each day and identify any hazards, safety concerns or maintenance requirements. Records of each check must be maintained and signed by the Nominated Supervisor or the Responsible Person in day-to-day charge of the service.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice. 

Waikerie Primary School OSHC (SE-00011092)

Address: 23 Coombe Terrace, Waikerie SA 5330

Approved Provider: Waikerie Primary School Council Inc (PR-00006436)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165 (1) – Offence to inadequately supervise children 
  • Section 167(1) - Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazard.
  • Regulation 93 (1)(b)– Administration of medication 
  • Regulation 102F – Children disembarking a means of transport – centre-based service 

Action Taken: 
Issue of Emergency Action Notice (Section 179 of the National Law) on 28 August 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. The service must immediately cease providing education and care to children who require prescribed mediation unless the following is available at the service:
    a. A current medical management plan.
    b. Prescribed medication, bearing the pharmacy label identifying the child for whom it is prescribed, and within expiry. 
    c. Risk minimisation and communication plans.
  2. The service must operate with a minimum of two educators at all times while providing education and care. 
  3. The Approved Provider must notify all families of children enrolled at the service of the directions in this notice and its implications for service operations. The Approved Provider must provide the ESB with a copy of the communication and evidence that it has been distributed to all enrolled families.
  4. The service must ensure that a documented procedure is developed and implemented for the arrival of children transported to the service by bus, in accordance with the requirements under Regulation 102F. This procedure must clearly outline: 
    a) How children are supervised while disembarking the bus.
    b) How the service confirms each child’s arrival.
    c) How the service confirms no children remain within the bus.
    d) How the children are transitioned safely into the OSHC environment.
    e) How the records of the above are maintained.
    f) The roles and responsibilities of the service staff during this process.
  5. Provide evidence that the Medical Conditions policy is implemented. Evidence must include records demonstrating that, for every child diagnosed with a medical condition: 
    a) A current medical management plan is available.
    b) Prescribed medication is available in its original packaging, correctly labelled and within its expiry date.
    c) Risk minimisation and communication plans are available.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Emergency Action Notice. 

The Ranges Early Learning and Care Centre – Stirling (SE -00011046)

Address: 137 Mount Barker Road STIRLING SA 5152

Approved Provider: RANGES ELC PTY LTD (PR- 40037090)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Regulation 103 – Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair

Action Taken: 
Issue of Emergency Action Notice (Section 179 of the National Law) on 10 September 2026 requiring the following by a specified time:

  1. Provide communication to the families of all children enrolled at the service of the observations and actions outlined in this notice.
  2. The Approved Provider must provide evidence of the communication outlined above to the ESB. 
  3. The Approved Provider must conduct and document a risk assessment of all areas identified in this notice and immediately implement appropriate control measures, including restricting access to affected areas, removing hazardous items, or ceasing use of affected premises, furniture or equipment, to ensure children are not exposed to risks pending remediation and rectification.
  4. The Approved Provider must engage a suitably qualified contractor to undertake a comprehensive assessment of the identified mould, water damage and affected building surfaces within the service. The assessment must identify:
    a. the extent of the mould, moisture and water damage.
    b. the source or contributing factors associated with the mould, moisture and water damage.
    c. all remediation and rectification works required to address the identified mould, moisture and water damage and any contributing factors.
    d. whether any affected area is safe for use by children pending completion of the identified remediation and rectification works.
    e. the timeframe for completion of all identified remediation and rectification works.
  5. Engage a suitably qualified professional cleaning contractor to undertake a comprehensive clean of all areas identified as not being maintained in a clean and hygienic condition, including the removal of cobwebs and spider activity and the cleaning of walls, café blinds, furniture and surfaces, to ensure the premises, furniture and equipment are maintained in a clean and hygienic condition.
  6. Implement all remediation and rectification works identified by the suitably qualified contractor that are necessary to address the identified mould, moisture and water damage and any contributing factors, and to ensure the affected premises and building surfaces are safe, clean and in good repair. Where the contractor's assessment identifies that an affected area is not safe for use by children, prevent children from accessing that area until all required remediation and rectification works have been completed and the area is determined to be safe for use.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Emergency Action Notice. 

Kingston Early Learning Centre (SE-00010539) 

Address: 32 Cameron Street, KINGSTON SE, SA 5275

Approved Provider: Department for Education (PR-00006069)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Regulation 155(c) - Interactions with children - maintains at all times the dignity and rights of each child 
  • Regulation 103 - Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 24 August 2026 requiring the following by a specified time:

  1. Cease the practice of photographing children in circumstances that do not adequately maintain their dignity and rights, and implement controls governing the taking, storage and management of photographs of children, including to ensure that:
    a) children are not photographed while unclothed or in circumstances that unnecessarily expose intimate areas of their body
    b) where a child is injured, distressed or requires first aid, the child's immediate care, comfort, reassurance and medical needs are prioritised before consideration is given to taking any photograph
    c) photographs are only taken where there is a legitimate service-related purpose and in a manner that maintains the child's dignity, privacy and rights
    d) existing photographs stored on service devices are reviewed and any photographs that are not required to be retained for a lawful or legitimate service-related purpose are securely deleted in accordance with applicable record-keeping requirements
    e) all educators and staff are provided with clear instruction regarding the circumstances in which photographs of children may and may not be taken, including the appropriate management of photographs relating to injuries or incidents
    f) management oversight is implemented to ensure ongoing compliance with the service's requirements for the taking, storage and management of photographs of children.
  2. Remove the accumulation of leaf litter and organic debris from the sandpit and implement a documented cleaning and maintenance schedule to ensure the sandpit and outdoor play areas are regularly inspected, cleaned and maintained in a safe and hygienic condition.
  3. Remove the PVC piping from the ground within the outdoor play area and store it in a manner that prevents the accumulation or concealment of debris, pests and other hazards and prevents unintended access by children.
  4. Remove the unstable outdoor picnic table from the service premises.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice.

The Heights Primary School OSHC (SE-00011032)

Address: Brunel Drive, Modbury Heights 5092, SA 

Approved Provider: The Heights School Council Inc (PR-00006408)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165(1) – Offence to inadequately supervise children 
  • Section 167(1) – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazard
  • Section 172(1)(a)(b)(d) – Offence to fail to display prescribed information 
  • Section 173(1)(b)(2)(b) – Offence to fail to notify certain circumstances to Regulatory Authority
  • Regulation 84C – Risk assessment for purposes of sleep and rest policies and procedures
  • Regulation 92(3)(b) – Medication Record
  • Regulation 95(a) – Procedure for administration of medication
  • Regulation 97(2)(3)(a)(b) – Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 168(2)(a)(v)(j) – Education and care service must have policies and procedures
  • Regulation 170(1) – Policies and procedures to be followed

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 8 May 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Provide evidence that supervision procedures are being implemented consistently, with strengthened arrangements to ensure all children remain within sight and hearing of an educator at all times (including during transitions, outdoor play, access to belongings, and arrival from school-based duties). Evidence must include updated supervision procedures and an educator positioning plan/supervision map demonstrating how children are prevented from moving between areas unsupervised.
  2. Provide evidence that all OSHC staff have received external supervision training aligned with the service’s supervision policy, including attendance records and individual reflections.
  3. Provide evidence that risk controls have been implemented to protect children from foreseeable harm and hazards across all playground/outdoor environments. Evidence must include a current risk assessment for outdoor environments and documented controls to prevent unsafe access to high risk structures (including how controls will be monitored and enforced).
  4. Provide evidence that all prescribed information (including the provider approval, service approval, and current National Quality Standard rating) is displayed in a location that is clearly visible and accessible to families and visitors at all times.
  5. Provide evidence that notification processes have been strengthened to ensure prescribed changes to Persons with Management or Control and Nominated Supervisors are identified and notified to the Regulatory Authority within the required timeframes. Evidence must include the updated procedure/checklist and confirmation that current PMC and Nominated Supervisor details have been accurately lodged or updated through NQA ITS.
  6. Provide evidence that all required policies and procedures are in place, up to date, accessible to educators, and available at the service at all times. This must include the sleep and rest policy, the interactions with children policy, and the delivery and collection procedure for children returning from school based duties or activities (including crossing duty). Evidence must include confirmation that the current versions have been implemented and communicated to staff.
  7. Provide evidence that a sleep and rest risk assessment has been developed and implemented to support the service’s sleep and rest policies and procedures, including that risks associated with sleep and rest practices have been identified, assessed, and addressed.
  8. Provide evidence that the Medical Conditions Policy is implemented consistently. Evidence must include updated procedures/checklists and compliant samples demonstrating that:
    - all medical management plans and risk minimisation plans are current, authorised, and include all required information (including photographs where required);
    - risk minimisation plans identify clear controls and strategies to minimise risks (not solely listing medication); and
    - medication is signed in by families as required.
  9. Provide evidence that all medication held on site is current, within expiry, and suitable for use, and that a process is in place to identify, remove, and replace expired medication promptly. Evidence must include the medication register/check process and confirmation that any expired medication has been removed and replaced.
  10. Provide evidence that an emergency risk assessment has been developed and implemented, identifying and assessing the types of potential emergencies that may occur at the service.
  11. Provide evidence that the Emergency Procedures Policy is being followed, including that evacuation, invacuation, and lockdown rehearsals have been conducted within the last three months and that each rehearsal has been documented and evaluated. Evidence must include the most recent drill records (date, scenario, attendance, and debrief/evaluation) and a schedule of rehearsals demonstrating how the service will ensure these are conducted at least every three months.
  12. Provide evidence that evacuation diagrams and instructions are displayed at each exit in the OSHC main room and are visible and accessible to educators, children, and visitors.
  13. Implement a structured induction process for all new OSHC employees that includes training on all current service policies, procedures and risk assessments; a competency assessment to demonstrate understanding and application of training; and a signed register confirming each new employee has completed the required training and has been assessed as competent. Provide evidence of this process, including an example of the induction training program and an example of the competency/register documentation used by the service
  14. Implement annual refresher training for all employees that address all current service policies, procedures and risk assessments. Provide evidence of this, including an example of the refresher training program and documentation on how completion is recorded.  

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice.

  • Regulation 97(3)(a) — Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 97(3)(b) — Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 155 (c) and (d) - Interactions with children

    c. engaging a suitably qualified external provider to deliver face-to-face training to all educators on active supervision, including educator positioning, scanning and monitoring, responding to hazards and unsafe behaviours, maintaining effective supervision in outdoor environments and intervening promptly where children are exposed to risk
    d. ensuring all educators complete the training and demonstrate competency in implementing the service's supervision procedures
    e. implementing monitoring and oversight processes to verify supervision practices are consistently implemented and remain effective.
  2. Eliminate climbing and environmental hazards by:
    a. replacing the section of internal barrier fencing adjacent to the external boundary fence with non-climbable fencing that prevents children from climbing the fence or using it to gain access to the external boundary fence and adjoining restricted railway corridor
    b. engaging a suitably qualified person to undertake a comprehensive assessment of the outdoor environment to identify hazards that may expose children to injury or harm, including climbing opportunities, fixed infrastructure, boundary fencing and loose equipment
    c. implementing all recommendations arising from the assessment to eliminate or minimise identified hazards
    d. develop and implement procedures to routinely inspect, monitor and maintain environmental risk controls to ensure they remain effective in eliminating or minimising risks to children's safety, health and wellbeing.
  3. Implement procedures to ensure educator interactions with children are respectful, appropriate and responsive by:
    a. reviewing educator practices relating to behaviour guidance, supervision and interactions with children to identify deficiencies.
    b. implementing procedures that ensure educators respond promptly and appropriately when children engage in unsafe behaviours or require support or intervention
    c. providing guidance and training to educators regarding respectful interactions and behaviour guidance consistent with the national law, national regulations and the approved learning framework
    d. monitoring educator practices through regular observation and supervision to ensure compliance is maintained.
  4. Implement procedures to ensure complaints alleging that the safety, health or wellbeing of a child has been or is being compromised are managed in accordance with the National Law by:
    a. reviewing and, where necessary, revising the service's complaint management procedures to ensure complaints are promptly identified, escalated and assessed
    b. ensuring all complaints alleging that the safety, health or wellbeing of a child has been or may have been compromised are immediately referred to the approved provider or nominated supervisor for assessment
    c. implementing procedures to assess whether complaints are required to be notified to the regulatory authority under the national law.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice.

Address: Corner Lincoln Highway & Heurich Terrace, Whyalla, SA, 5608 
Approved Provider: Adelaide Christian Schools Inc (PR-0006395)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165 (1) – Offence to inadequately supervise children 
  • Section 167(1) - Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazard.
  • Regulation 93 (1)(b)– Administration of medication 
  • Regulation 97 (2) – Emergency and evacuation procedures 
  • Regulation 97 (3)(a) – Emergency and evacuation procedures 
  • Regulation 170 – Policies and procedures to be followed

Action Taken: 
Issue of Emergency Action Notice (Section 179 of the National Law) on, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. The service must immediately cease providing education and care to any child who requires prescribed medication unless the following are available and current at the service:
    a. a current Medical Management Plan;
    b. prescribed medication in its original packaging, bearing the pharmacy label identifying the child for whom it is prescribed, and within its expiry date; and
    c. where required, a current Risk Minimisation Plan and Communication Plan.
  2. The service must ensure a minimum educator-to-child ratio of 1:10 is maintained at all times while providing education and care.
  3. Provide written communication to the families of all children who require prescribed medication outlining the requirements of Action 1 and advising that children must not attend the service unless the required documentation and medication are available at the service.
  4. Provide evidence that supervision procedures have been reviewed and implemented to ensure all children remain within the sight and/or hearing of an educator at all times, including during transitions, outdoor play and access to bathroom facilities. Evidence must include updated supervision procedures and an educator deployment plan/supervision map demonstrating how children are prevented from moving between areas unsupervised.

    a. a current Medical Management Plan is available;
    b. prescribed medication is available in its original packaging, correctly labelled and within its expiry date; and
    c. where required, a current Risk Minimisation Plan and Communication Plan are available.
  6. Provide evidence that an emergency risk assessment has been developed and implemented and communicated to educators identifying and assessing the types of potential emergencies that may occur at the service.
  7. Conduct and document emergency evacuation, invacuation and lockdown rehearsals. Evidence must include the date, scenario, participating educators and children, evaluation of the effectiveness of each rehearsal, and any improvements identified. The service must thereafter ensure rehearsals continue to be conducted and documented at least once every three months.

Playford Primary School OSHC (SE-00018224)

Address: 216 Adams Road, Craigmore SA 5114 
Approved Provider: Playford Primary School Council Inc (PR-00011445)

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Section 165 – Offence to inadequately supervise children 
  • Section 172 – Offence to fail to display prescribed information
  • Regulation 87 – Incident, injury, trauma and illness record
  • Regulation 92 – Medication Record
  • Regulation 93 – Administration of medication 
  • Regulation 97 – Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 170 – Policies and procedures to be followed
  • Regulation 181 – Confidentiality of records kept by approved provider

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 3 July 2026, requiring the following by a specified time: 

  1. Provide evidence that supervision procedures are being implemented consistently, with strengthened arrangements to ensure all children remain within sight and hearing of an educator at all times (including during transitions, outdoor play, access to belongings, and signing in). Evidence must include updated supervision procedures and an educator positioning plan/supervision map demonstrating how children are prevented from moving between areas unsupervised.
  2. Provide evidence that all OSHC staff have received external supervision training aligned with the service’s supervision policy, including attendance records and individual reflections.
  3. Provide evidence that an incident, injury, trauma and illness record has been implemented that contains all the required fields. Evidence must also demonstrate how records are stored to maintain confidentiality and prevent unauthorised access. 
  4. Provide evidence that the Managing Medical Conditions Policy is implemented consistently. Evidence must include records for all children enrolled with a medical condition demonstrating that:
    a. parent/guardian authorisation to administer medication has been provided;
    b. risk minimisation plans are current and identify clear controls and strategies to minimise risks; and
    c. communication plans are developed in consultation with parents.
  5. Provide evidence that all medication held on site is current, within expiry, and suitable for use, and that a process is in place to identify, remove, and replace expired medication promptly. Evidence must include the medication register/check process and confirmation that any expired medication has been removed and replaced.
  6. Provide evidence that all prescribed information is displayed, including:
    a) the hours and days of operation of the education and care service;
    b) the name and telephone number of the person to whom complaints may be addressed;
    c) the name of the educational leader at the service;
    d) the contact details of the Regulatory Authority; and
    e) a notice stating that a child who has been diagnosed as at risk of anaphylaxis is enrolled at the service
  7. provide evidence that the Emergency and Evacuation Policy is being followed, including that evacuation, invacuation, and lockdown rehearsals have been conducted within the last three months and that each rehearsal has been documented and evaluated. Evidence must include confirmation that rehearsals will continue to be conducted at least every three months.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice

  3. Undertake a risk assessment of the uneven wooden walkway adjacent to the sandpit and develop a plan to complete works that ensure the surface is level, stable and safe for children.
  4. Ensure hoses are secured and stored when not in use. Provide evidence.
  5. Repair the iron flange beneath the exterior window to remove sharp edges.
  6. Undertake an audit of all internal doors and exit points to ensure appropriate controls are in place to prevent unauthorised egress by children.
  7. Install a lockable gate or door between the children’s indoor area and the kitchen.
  8. Replace the affected decking to ensure the surface is maintained in a safe condition and does not present a risk of injury.
  9. Replace the torn shade sail on the northwest side of the yard.
  10. Repair the damaged flooring and clean and maintain the floor drain to ensure the bathroom area is maintained in a safe, clean and hygienic condition.
  11. Provide a copy of the current Medical Conditions Policy.
  12. Provide updated enrolment records for each enrolled child that include the name, address and telephone number of the registered medical practitioner or medical service.

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice

  2. Advise parents that children requiring prescribed medication must not attend the service unless a current medical management or action plan completed by a registered medical practitioner has been provided.

  6. Remove unstable equipment in the outdoor environment, including wooden boat structures in poor repair. 
  7. Conduct a comprehensive deep clean and sanitisation of the kindergarten bathroom to address hygiene risks and implement measures to ensure the area is continually maintained to an appropriate hygienic standard, providing evidence to the ESB.
  8. Engage a suitably qualified contractor to assess and address the water damage and mold identified in the kindergarten bathroom, and ensure the area is maintained in a clean and hygienic condition. Provide a copy of the report with actions and risk mitigations to the ESB. 
  9. Implement immediate measures to eliminate identified egress risks associated with the stone retaining wall in the kindergarten outdoor area to prevent children exiting the premises or sustaining injury. 
  10. Provide a current sleep and rest risk assessment and emergency risk assessment that identifies foreseeable hazards and outlines risk control measures to ensure children’s safety. 
  11. Ensure the outdoor environment contains safe and age-appropriate resources to support safe play and supervision.

Murray Bridge World of Learning (SE-00011057)

  • Section 165 (1) – Offence to inadequately supervise children 
  • Section 167(1) - Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazard.

Action Taken:
Pursuant to section 55 of the National Law the following conditions were imposed on to the service’s Approval from 17 February 2026.

  1. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service must not provide education and care to more than 67 children at a time. 
  2. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service must operate with one (1) educator above the minimum educator-to-child ratio requirements across rooms with children aged 12 months or older at all times. 
  3. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, one of either the Service Director or Assistant Director must be onsite each day in a non-contact capacity. This person must not be included in educator-to-child ratio calculations at any time. 
  4. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service must conduct and record headcounts in each room at intervals not exceeding 15 minutes while children are present at the service. 

Outcome 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Compliance Notice

  • Regulation 97 - Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Section 167 – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazard.

Action Taken:

  1. Provide communication to the families of all children enrolled of the service, advising that:
    a. A child requiring prescribed medication must not attend the service where the dosage in the medication plan is inconsistent with the dosage stated on the pharmacy label.
    b. The service is required to undertake the actions set out in this notice. 
  2. Engage a contractor to conduct a review of the service’s emergency and evacuation plans and procedures, and, establish a clearly designated evacuation route that is displayed at each exit, including the designated evacuation routes and assembly points. 
  3. Provide a 12-month schedule for emergency evacuation rehearsals.

Outcome:

Pursuant to Section 55 of the National Law, the following conditions were imposed on to the Service’s Approval from 13 April 2026 

  1. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service must not provide education and care to more than 13 children at a time. 
  2. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service must not accept any new enrolments or casual booking requests. 
  3. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service is restricted to operating within two adjoining rooms sharing a bathroom and outdoor space. 
  4. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service is to not provide care to children who require additional support.
  5. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service is not to provide education and care to children with known allergens, intolerances or dietary requirements.
  6. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service is not to provide education and care to children with known medical conditions.
  7. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the Nominated Supervisor must be present at the service and excluded from ratio calculations and must operate in a non contact capacity.
  8. Until a date determined by the Regulatory Authority, the service must operate with an educator not included within ratio to supervise daily operations within each room. The educator will be responsible for the following tasks:
    - Conducting and digitally recording headcounts every 15 minutes.
    - Conducting and recording 10-minute sleep checks.

  5. Remove all loose timber and hazardous materials from the alloy dinghy in the sandpit. 
  6. Ensure enrolment records are available and accessible at the service. 
  7. Develop a Quality Improvement Plan. 
  8. Develop, implement and make available an educational program. 
  9. Amend the sleep and rest policy to address supervision and monitoring requirements. 
  10. Conduct and document a sleep and rest risk assessment. 
  11. Develop and implement required medical management plans for child BWW. 
  12. Ensure all medication is stored in original labelled packaging with supporting documentation. 
  13. Conduct a full medication audit to ensure compliance with storage, documentation and medical management requirements. 
  14. Develop and implement a delivery and collection of children policy. 
  15. Develop and implement an interactions with children policy. 
  16. Conduct and document an emergency evacuation and invacuation drill. 
  17. Develop a plan to conduct emergency evacuation and invacuation drills from the issuance of this notice until 30 June 2027. 
  18. Display all prescribed information as required under the National Regulations. 

Outcome:

Edge Early Learning South Australia 

Compliance Type: Enforceable Undertaking                                                                                         

Approved Provider: Edge Early Learning South Australia Pty Ltd

Approved Provider Address: 70 Cordelia Street, South Brisbane QLD 4101

Reason for Enforceable Undertaking: 
The Regulatory Authority alleges that Edge Early Learning has failed to comply with section 19(2) of the National Law, which makes it a condition of a provider approval that the approved provider must comply with the law.

Date of agreed Undertaking: 
28 August 2025 

Details of Enforceable Undertaking: 
For a period of 12 months, through to 28 August 2026, Edge Early Learning undertakes to address the following key areas:

  • Approved Provider Leadership and Oversight
  • Staff Recruitment, Induction and Ongoing Professional Development
  • Educator Conduct and Children’s Safety
  • Edge Early Learning Service and Undertaking Governance

Edge Early Learning undertakes to: 

  1. Implement an organisational structure to have one (1) Area Manager to five (5) services.
  2. Require Area Managers to undertake a visit to each service they are responsible for at least once per fortnight. 
  3. Hold quarterly all-staff meetings, either in person or virtually, to provide a comprehensive overview of operational compliance. These sessions are to be led by the Chief Executive Officer or Head of Quality, Safety and Wellbeing, who outline key themes of non-compliance and present actions for improvement across South Australian services. 
  4. Within six weeks of the undertaking commencing, Edge is to engage an external organisational psychologist to undertake a staff engagement survey, focusing what staff believe they need in order to deliver quality and safe education and care. 
  5. Within twelve weeks of the undertaking commencing, Edge is to provide the ESB with a copy of the organisational psychologist’s report and staff engagement survey results, as well as an action plan to deliver against. 
  6. Within four weeks of the undertaking commencing, Edge is to engage an external consultant to review and within twelve weeks of the undertaking commencing, provide updated policies and procedures on recruitment, induction and ongoing professional development. 
  7. Edge is to provide the ESB with a monthly register of all staff employed by Edge at services in South Australia. 
  8. Commencing four weeks after the Undertaking commencing, on a quarterly basis Edge is to provide evidence to the ESB of induction and ongoing professional development training provided to staff.
  9. Implement a reduced ratio of 1:9 children for children aged 36-months up to and including preschool age. 
  10. Within two weeks of the undertaking commencing, the Head of Quality, Safety and Wellbeing will complete a review and update of the policy and procedure on supervision and head counts. This will be implemented within six weeks of the policy and procedure being finalised. 
  11. Within four weeks of the undertaking commencing, Edge is to ensure induction of new staff includes food safety training as well as a bi-annual refresher. Evidence of implementation is to be provided to the ESB.
  12. Ensure that where a child has a known allergen, the Nominated Supervisor or Responsible Person observes and signs off on the child being provided with the correct food, with monthly reporting provided to the ESB. 
  13. Not apply to open or transfer services in South Australia for the duration of the undertaking (Twelve Months). 
  14. Meet with the ESB monthly, to discuss performance against the undertaking. 
  15. Notify the ESB in writing, within 48 hours, if it becomes aware of any matter which will have the effect that Edge is not able to comply with any condition of the undertaking. 
  16. Understand that the ESB reserves the right to conduct inspections at any Edge service during the Term of the undertaking in accordance with the National Law and National Regulations and take action proportionate to the risk presented.

Steps taken: 
To support Edge Early Learning’s ongoing compliance with the National Law and Regulations, the Regulatory Authority has accepted a written undertaking voluntarily provided by Edge Early Learning in line with the terms above and will monitor compliance with this undertaking over its 12-month duration. 

Failure to comply with the undertaking may result in the Regulatory Authority pursuing further enforcement action, including prosecution.

  14. Implement compliant incident reporting processes and ensure all staff are trained in their use.
  15. Fully restock all first aid kits and remove all out-of-date items.
  16. Conduct a full medication audit and implement compliant medication management practices, including:
    - Updating the medication policy.
    - Developing and implementing required communication plans, action plans, and risk minimisation plans for all children requiring medication; and
    - Ensuring out-of-date medication is removed and not stored with current medication.
  17. Review and update emergency and evacuation plans and procedures and conduct and document emergency and evacuation drills within prescribed timeframes.
  18. Ensure effective and immediate communication is available at all times in all care rooms.
  19. Review and update all policies and procedures to ensure they are current and subject to regular review.
  20. Display all prescribed information, including hours and days of operation, Educational Leader details, complaints contact information, and ESB contact details.

Outcome:

     - Evidence has been provided that an induction on kitchen procedures and safe cooking practices for all staff who may be required to work in the  kitchen at any time has been provided. 
  2. Implement a reduced educator to child ratio of 1:7 in the pre-kindy and preschool rooms.

Outcome: 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Emergency Action Notice.

Outcome: 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Emergency Action Notice.

il nido Children's Centres for Early Childhood Development and Parenting

Address: 22 Campbell Road, Paradise SA 5075

Approved Provider: Ms Melissa Gobell (PR-00006151) 

Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board (ESB) is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Regulation 168(ha) – The service must implement a policy and procedure for the safe use of digital technologies and online environments, including controls on personal devices, to ensure children’s safety, health and wellbeing.

Action Taken: 
Pursuant to section 55 of the National Law the following conditions were imposed on to the service’s Approval from 16 October requiring the following by 29 October 2025.

  1. Make a daily record of all mobile devices on site, inclusive of wearable devices, that each have the capability to capture, share or store images of children. The record must include the owner of each device, whether it is service-owned or a personal device, how each will be used and where each device will be during the day, including where it is stored if not in use. 
  2. Submit to the ESB, at the end of each week, the daily records of mobile devices for the preceding week. 
  3. Develop and implement a policy and procedure addressing the use of personal devices, in accordance with the National Model Code. 
  4. Submit the assurance to the ESB within 14 days of the date of the conditions being applied that the service has implemented a policy and procedure for the use of personal devices. 

Outcome: 
The Approved Provider implemented a policy and procedure for the safe use of digital technologies and the conditions were removed.

  3. The service must operate with two (2) staff members above minimum requirements as per the timeframe stipulated within the notice. 

Outcome: 
The Approved Provider carried out the actions outlined in the Emergency Action Notice.

Victoria Park Child Care Centre (SE-00011086)

Address: 163 Fullarton Road, Rose Park SA 5067

Approved Provider: Mr George Yannis & Mrs Thelma Yannis (PR-00006461)
 
Details: 
A Delegate of the Education Standards Board is satisfied that the Approved Provider has contravened: 

  • Regulation 77 – Health hygiene and safe food practices
  • Regulation 78 – Food and beverages
  • Regulation 84 – Awareness of child protection law
  • Regulation 84A – Sleep and rest
  • Regulation 84C – Risk assessment for purposes of sleep and rest policies and procedures
  • Regulation 89 – First aid kits
  • Regulation 97 – Emergency and evacuation procedures
  • Regulation 102AAC – Risk assessment for purposes of safe arrival of children policies and procedures
  • Regulation 103 – Premises, furniture and equipment to be safe, clean and in good repair
  • Regulation 105 – Furniture, materials and equipment
  • Regulation 109 – Toilet and hygiene facilities
  • Regulation 110 – Ventilation and natural light
  • Regulation 126 – Centre-based services—general educator qualifications
  • Regulation 132 – Requirement for early childhood teacher
  • Regulation 136 – First aid qualifications
  • Regulation 145 – Staff record
  • Regulation 146 – Nominated supervisor
  • Regulation 147 – Staff members
  • Regulation 148 – Educational leader
  • Regulation 152 – Record of access to early childhood teachers
  • Regulation 168 – Education and care service must have policies and procedures
  • Regulation 173 – Prescribed information to be displayed
  • Section 162A – Child protection training
  • Section 167(1) – Offence relating to protection of children from harm and hazards
  • Section 169(1) – Offence relating to staffing arrangements

Action Taken: 
Issue of Compliance Notice (Section 177 of the National Law) on 10 July 2025, requiring the following by 31 July 2025 

Please provide documentation demonstrating compliance with the matters listed below. 

Physical Environment 

  1. Cords in the Baby Sleep Room are secured and out of children’s reach. 
  2. Improved lighting and visibility to the sleep room. 
  3. Evacuation diagrams are displayed near all exit points, showing paths of egress. 
  4. First Aid kits are adequately stocked and accessible in both indoor and outdoor areas. 
  5. All areas of the service have sufficient natural lighting and ventilation. 
  6. A project plan and timelines for establishing appropriate ventilation and natural light measures in the baby sleep room, toddler room 2 and kindy room. 
  7. Clutter is removed from both indoor and outdoor environments. 
  8. The carpet in the Baby Sleep Room has been repaired. 
  9. Sandpits meet the required minimum depth of 500mm. 
  10. The risk posed by the exposed pipe on the Kindy Room rear wall has been mitigated. 
  11. Animal feed is stored securely and cannot be accessed by children. 
  12. Paper/board used to conceal damaged walls, doors or windows has been removed, and a maintenance plan and risk assessment are in place. 
  13. Commercial paint has been removed from all licensed areas. 
  14. The mosquito net above the gate between Toddler Room 1 and 2 has been removed. 
  15. Hazards within reach of children at the Kindy Room gate have been addressed and removed. 
  16. The portable animal enclosure in the yard has been removed or adequately repaired.
  17. Mats in the Baby Room are clean and in good repair. 
  18. The current contact details for the Education Standards Board are displayed alongside the Prescribed Information at the service entry. 

Risk Management 

  1. A current Sleep and Rest Risk Minimisation Plan is in place. 
  2. A schedule for emergency rehearsals is established from the date of this notice to 30 July 2026. 
  3. A general risk assessment identifying potential emergencies has been completed. 
  4. A risk assessment relating to animals within the premises is in place. 
  5. A risk assessment and mitigation strategies address supervision of children in toilet areas. 
  6. A risk assessment and mitigation strategies for supervision of the baby sleep room. 
  7. A risk assessment and risk mitigation measures are in place for the broken decking in the children’s yard. 
  8. Daily yard checks are conducted by educators prior to outdoor access. 

Policies and Procedures 

  1. All policies and procedures required under Regulation 168(2) have been reviewed and are current. 
  2. Medical Management Plans, Risk Minimisation Plans, and Communication Plans are in place for all children with a diagnosed health care need, allergy, or medical condition (Regulation 90). 
  3. Strategies are implemented to ensure compliance with Regulations 77–80 (health, hygiene, food and beverage provisions). 
  4. The staff induction process includes appropriate information and guidance on Regulations 77, 78, 79, and 90. 

Staffing and Records 

  1. Efforts made to recruit an Early Childhood Teacher (ECT) are documented (e.g., advertisements, correspondence).
  2. Complete staff records are maintained in accordance with regulation 147, including: 
    - Working With Children Checks
    - Qualifications
    - Completed training in First Aid, Asthma, Anaphylaxis, and Child Protection (as per Regulation 84)   

Outcome:
The Approved Provider surrendered their service approval and are no longer able to provide education and care.

Happy Haven OSHC Mark Oliphant College

Approved provider name:  PR- 40001361 HAPPY HAVEN OSHC PTY LTD

Address:  99 Douglas Drive, Munno Para West, SA 5115

Date of action:  4/05/2023

Type of action:  Amendment of service approval issued pursuant to s 55 of the National Law

Details of action:  The Registrar of the Education Standards Board issued the approved provider with an amendment of service approval.  The following conditions to service approval take immediate effect:

  • The service is to always operate with one qualified staff above minimum numbers required.
  • The service is to adhere to the current risk assessment ensuring that all treatment plans from the Emergency Action Notice and recent reviews are being followed.
  • The service must not take any new enrolments.
  • The service must not apply for waivers in relation to staffing.
  • The service must not provide care for preschool aged children.
  • The service engages an external early childhood consultant to conduct a review of service practices, policies and procedures and provide ongoing mentoring to the staff team in adequate supervision and supporting individual children’s needs through effective programming.
  • An action plan is developed, implemented and monitored by the approved provider and consultant based on the findings of the review.
  • Monthly meetings are held with the school principal and school governing council to report on progress of the action plan and discuss any incidents.

Date of action: 4/5/2020

Type of action: Enforceable undertaking pursuant to s 179(a) of the National Law

Details of action: The The Registrar of the Education Standards Board issued the approved provider with an enforceable undertaking in relation to failing to comply with provisions of the National Law and Regulations. The matters giving rise to the enforceable undertaking being issued included:

  • Provide an education and care service without service approval pursuant to s 103.

Black Forest Primary School OSHC

Approved provider: Black Forest Primary School Council Inc

Address: 679 South Road, BLACK FOREST, SA 5035

Date of action: 4/4/2022

Type of action: Compliance notice issued pursuant to s 177 of the National Law

Details of action: The Registrar of the Education Standards Board issued the approved provider with a compliance notice in relation to failing to comply with provisions of the National Law. The matters giving rise to the compliance notice being issued included:

  • Inadequate supervision of children pursuant to s 165 of the National Law
  • Protection of children from harm and hazards pursuant to s 167 of the National Law

The approved provider has provided evidence that they have complied with the provisions of the National Law and Regulations in relation to the compliance notice.
